--- SOURCE: mojo_samples/hft_latency.mojo ---
# hft_latency.mojo
#
# Original code written for the START project's Mojo code samples.
# Component 3 (HFT FRAMEWORKS): a latency-measurement harness.
#
# SIMULATOR ONLY. This file measures how long simulated matching, parsing
# and risk-checking operations take on this machine. It never connects to
# an exchange, a broker, or any market-data vendor, and no real money is
# involved anywhere.
#
# Standard library only. No external packages, no Python interop, no network.
#
# Why latency is measured this way (the ideas behind the harness):
#
#   * Wall-clock time is measured in nanoseconds with a monotonic counter
#     (perf_counter_ns). A monotonic counter never jumps when the system
#     clock is adjusted, so differences between two readings are real
#     elapsed time. One nanosecond is one billionth of a second; a modern
#     CPU executes roughly one simple instruction per nanosecond, so
#     nanoseconds are the honest unit for this work.
#   * A single operation is timed, not a batch average. Batch averages
#     hide the slow operations, and in trading the slow operations are
#     the ones that lose money. Every sample is kept, then summarised.
#   * The summary is a distribution, not a single number. The mean is
#     reported, but the mean lies: a thousand 100 ns operations and one
#     100,000 ns operation average to about 200 ns, which describes
#     neither group. Percentiles describe the distribution honestly:
#     p50 is the median (half the operations were faster), p99 is the
#     latency that 99% of operations beat (the "almost worst" case), and
#     p999 is the near-worst case. A venue that promises "under a
#     microsecond" is promising a p99, not a mean.
#   * A histogram shows the shape: how many operations landed in each
#     latency bucket. A healthy harness shows one tight cluster; a
#     second cluster far to the right usually means garbage collection,
#     a context switch, or a cache miss.
#   * Warmup matters. The first few operations pay for cold caches and
#     just-in-time compilation, so the harness runs untimed warmup
#     iterations before it starts recording. Without warmup the p99 is
#     dominated by startup effects that never recur in steady state.
#   * The empty-loop benchmark measures the measurement itself: two
#     counter reads plus a loop iteration. If the empty loop costs 40 ns
#     and the operation under test costs 60 ns, the operation really
#     costs about 20 ns of work. Every other number in this file should
#     be read with that floor in mind.
#   * Throughput is derived from the same samples: operations per second
#     is (number of operations) / (total time). It is the inverse view of
#     the same data, useful when sizing how many orders a gateway can
#     absorb.
#   * Determinism: the workloads are generated from a fixed seed, so two
#     runs of this program exercise the same operations in the same
#     order. The absolute nanosecond values will still differ between
#     machines and runs (that is the nature of timing), but the structure
#     -- sample counts, percentile ordering, histogram totals, and the
#     comparison-count advantage of binary search over linear search --
#     is asserted exactly, because it does not depend on the clock.
#
# What this file benchmarks (all on simulated data):
#   1. Level lookup: linear scan vs binary search in a 1,000-level book.
#      The comparison counts are deterministic and asserted; the timings
#      are reported for the reader to see the constant-factor story.
#   2. Top-of-book matching: a market order consuming part of the best
#      level, restored after every timed operation.
#   3. A multi-level sweep: a market order consuming fifteen levels,
#      with the book rebuilt after every timed operation.
#   4. Text tick parsing: splitting and converting a wire message.
#   5. A risk-check pass: the integer arithmetic of quantity, notional
#      and price-band checks.
#   6. The empty loop: the measurement floor.
#
# What this file does NOT do (and says so honestly): it does not claim
# these numbers are what a production venue would see. A benchmark on a
# shared virtual machine with an untuned allocator is a teaching tool,
# not a service-level agreement. The harness is the deliverable; the
# numbers are an example of its output.

from std.testing import assert_equal, assert_true, assert_almost_equal
from std.time import perf_counter_ns
from std.math import sqrt
from std.random import seed, random_ui64
from std.builtin.sort import sort

comptime NS_PER_SEC = 1_000_000_000


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# LatencyStats: a collected distribution of nanosecond samples.
#
# Usage: add() samples in any order, then call finalize() once, which
# sorts the samples in place. Percentiles, min/max and the histogram all
# read the sorted order; mean and standard deviation do not need it.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

struct LatencyStats:
    var label: String
    var samples: List[Int]
    var sorted: Bool

    def __init__(out self, label: String):
        self.label = label
        self.samples = List[Int]()
        self.sorted = False

    def add(mut self, ns: Int):
        self.samples.append(ns)
        self.sorted = False

    def count(self) -> Int:
        return len(self.samples)

    def finalize(mut self):
        if not self.sorted:
            sort(self.samples)
            self.sorted = True

    def min_ns(mut self) -> Int:
        self.finalize()
        if len(self.samples) == 0:
            return 0
        return self.samples[0]

    def max_ns(mut self) -> Int:
        self.finalize()
        if len(self.samples) == 0:
            return 0
        return self.samples[len(self.samples) - 1]

    def mean_ns(self) -> Float64:
        if len(self.samples) == 0:
            return 0.0
        var total: Float64 = 0.0
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.samples):
            total += Float64(self.samples[i])
            i += 1
        return total / Float64(len(self.samples))

    # Nearest-rank percentile: the smallest sample such that at least p
    # percent of samples are <= it. p50 of [10,20,30,40,50] is 30.
    def percentile(mut self, p: Float64) -> Int:
        self.finalize()
        var n = len(self.samples)
        if n == 0:
            return 0
        var target = p * Float64(n) / 100.0
        var rank = Int(target)
        if Float64(rank) < target:
            rank += 1
        if rank < 1:
            rank = 1
        if rank > n:
            rank = n
        return self.samples[rank - 1]

    def p50(mut self) -> Int:
        return self.percentile(50.0)

    def p90(mut self) -> Int:
        return self.percentile(90.0)

    def p99(mut self) -> Int:
        return self.percentile(99.0)

    def p999(mut self) -> Int:
        return self.percentile(99.9)

    def stddev_ns(self) -> Float64:
        if len(self.samples) == 0:
            return 0.0
        var mean = self.mean_ns()
        var acc: Float64 = 0.0
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.samples):
            var diff = Float64(self.samples[i]) - mean
            acc += diff * diff
            i += 1
        return sqrt(acc / Float64(len(self.samples)))

    def total_ns(self) -> Int:
        var total = 0
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.samples):
            total += self.samples[i]
            i += 1
        return total

    # Throughput implied by the samples: operations per second.
    def ops_per_sec(self) -> Float64:
        var total = self.total_ns()
        if total <= 0:
            return 0.0
        return Float64(len(self.samples)) * Float64(NS_PER_SEC) / Float64(total)

    # Histogram: counts per bucket. edges are the upper bounds of the
    # first buckets; the last bucket holds everything above the last edge.
    # With edges [10, 20, 30, 40] and samples [5, 15, 25, 35, 45] the
    # counts are [1, 1, 1, 1, 1].
    def histogram(mut self, edges: List[Int]) -> List[Int]:
        self.finalize()
        var counts = List[Int]()
        var b = 0
        while b < len(edges) + 1:
            counts.append(0)
            b += 1
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.samples):
            var v = self.samples[i]
            var bucket = len(edges)
            var e = 0
            while e < len(edges):
                if v <= edges[e]:
                    bucket = e
                    break
                e += 1
            counts[bucket] += 1
            i += 1
        return counts^

    def summary(mut self) -> String:
        var s = String("[") + self.label + String("] n=") + String(self.count())
        s = s + String(" mean=") + String(self.mean_ns()) + String("ns")
        s = s + String(" min=") + String(self.min_ns()) + String("ns")
        s = s + String(" p50=") + String(self.p50()) + String("ns")
        s = s + String(" p90=") + String(self.p90()) + String("ns")
        s = s + String(" p99=") + String(self.p99()) + String("ns")
        s = s + String(" p999=") + String(self.p999()) + String("ns")
        s = s + String(" max=") + String(self.max_ns()) + String("ns")
        s = s + String(" stddev=") + String(self.stddev_ns()) + String("ns")
        s = s + String(" throughput=") + String(self.ops_per_sec()) + String(" ops/s")
        return s


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# LevelBook: a minimal ask-side book used as the benchmark workload.
# Prices are integer ticks, strictly increasing; quantities are whole
# units. Two lookup strategies are implemented so the harness can compare
# them on identical data: a linear scan and a binary search. Both return
# (index, comparisons) so the algorithmic difference is measurable
# without any clock at all.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

struct LevelBook:
    var px: List[Int]
    var qty: List[Int]
    var init_px: List[Int]
    var init_qty: List[Int]

    def __init__(out self):
        self.px = List[Int]()
        self.qty = List[Int]()
        self.init_px = List[Int]()
        self.init_qty = List[Int]()

    def build(mut self, n_levels: Int, base: Int, step: Int, qty_each: Int):
        self.px = List[Int]()
        self.qty = List[Int]()
        self.init_px = List[Int]()
        self.init_qty = List[Int]()
        var i = 0
        while i < n_levels:
            var p = base + i * step
            self.px.append(p)
            self.qty.append(qty_each)
            self.init_px.append(p)
            self.init_qty.append(qty_each)
            i += 1

    # Restore the book to its built state (used outside timed regions).
    def refill(mut self):
        self.px = List[Int]()
        self.qty = List[Int]()
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.init_px):
            self.px.append(self.init_px[i])
            self.qty.append(self.init_qty[i])
            i += 1

    def n_levels(self) -> Int:
        return len(self.px)

    def total_qty(self) -> Int:
        var total = 0
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.qty):
            total += self.qty[i]
            i += 1
        return total

    # Index of the first level priced >= target, or len(px) if none.
    # Returns (index, comparisons made).
    def find_linear(self, target: Int) -> Tuple[Int, Int]:
        var comps = 0
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.px):
            comps += 1
            if self.px[i] >= target:
                return (i, comps)
            i += 1
        return (len(self.px), comps)

    def find_binary(self, target: Int) -> Tuple[Int, Int]:
        var comps = 0
        var lo = 0
        var hi = len(self.px)
        while lo < hi:
            var mid = (lo + hi) // 2
            comps += 1
            if self.px[mid] < target:
                lo = mid + 1
            else:
                hi = mid
        return (lo, comps)

    def insert_linear(mut self, price: Int, qty: Int):
        var idx, _comps = self.find_linear(price)
        if idx < len(self.px) and self.px[idx] == price:
            self.qty[idx] += qty
        else:
            self.px.insert(idx, price)
            self.qty.insert(idx, qty)

    def insert_binary(mut self, price: Int, qty: Int):
        var idx, _comps = self.find_binary(price)
        if idx < len(self.px) and self.px[idx] == price:
            self.qty[idx] += qty
        else:
            self.px.insert(idx, price)
            self.qty.insert(idx, qty)

    # Consume qty from the front of the book (best prices first).
    # Returns the quantity actually filled.
    def match_market(mut self, qty: Int) -> Int:
        var remaining = qty
        var filled = 0
        while remaining > 0 and len(self.px) > 0:
            var take = remaining
            if self.qty[0] < take:
                take = self.qty[0]
            self.qty[0] -= take
            remaining -= take
            filled += take
            if self.qty[0] == 0:
                var _dead_px = self.px.pop(0)
                var _dead_qty = self.qty.pop(0)
        return filled

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Workloads under test.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def _atoi(s: String) -> Int:
    var value = 0
    for b in s.bytes():
        value = value * 10 + (Int(b) - 48)
    return value


def parse_tick_fields(msg: String) -> Tuple[Int, Int]:
    var parts = msg.split("|")
    var price = _atoi(String(parts[5]))
    var qty = _atoi(String(parts[6]))
    return (price, qty)


def risk_check_inline(price: Int, qty: Int) -> Bool:
    if qty <= 0 or qty > 1000:
        return False
    if price <= 0 or price > 1_000_000:
        return False
    var band = 10000 * 5 // 100
    if price < 10000 - band or price > 10000 + band:
        return False
    if price * qty > 1_000_000:
        return False
    return True


def bench_find_linear(n_ops: Int, n_levels: Int) -> LatencyStats:
    var book = LevelBook()
    book.build(n_levels, 10000, 1, 10)
    seed(20261105)
    var w = 0
    while w < 1000:
        var _i1, _c1 = book.find_linear(10000 + Int(random_ui64(0, 1000)))
        w += 1
    var st = LatencyStats("find-linear")
    var i = 0
    while i < n_ops:
        var target = 10000 + Int(random_ui64(0, 1000))
        var t0 = perf_counter_ns()
        var _idx, _comps = book.find_linear(target)
        var dt = perf_counter_ns() - t0
        st.add(dt)
        i += 1
    return st^


def bench_find_binary(n_ops: Int, n_levels: Int) -> LatencyStats:
    var book = LevelBook()
    book.build(n_levels, 10000, 1, 10)
    seed(20261105)
    var w = 0
    while w < 1000:
        var _i1, _c1 = book.find_binary(10000 + Int(random_ui64(0, 1000)))
        w += 1
    var st = LatencyStats("find-binary")
    var i = 0
    while i < n_ops:
        var target = 10000 + Int(random_ui64(0, 1000))
        var t0 = perf_counter_ns()
        var _idx, _comps = book.find_binary(target)
        var dt = perf_counter_ns() - t0
        st.add(dt)
        i += 1
    return st^


def bench_match_top(n_ops: Int) raises -> LatencyStats:
    var book = LevelBook()
    book.build(200, 10000, 1, 10)
    var w = 0
    while w < 100:
        var _f = book.match_market(7)
        book.qty[0] += 7
        w += 1
    var st = LatencyStats("match-top")
    var filled_total = 0
    var i = 0
    while i < n_ops:
        var t0 = perf_counter_ns()
        var filled = book.match_market(7)
        var dt = perf_counter_ns() - t0
        st.add(dt)
        filled_total += filled
        book.qty[0] += 7
        i += 1
    assert_equal(filled_total, n_ops * 7)
    return st^


def bench_match_sweep(n_ops: Int) raises -> LatencyStats:
    var book = LevelBook()
    book.build(200, 10000, 1, 10)
    var w = 0
    while w < 10:
        var _f = book.match_market(150)
        book.refill()
        w += 1
    var st = LatencyStats("match-sweep-15-levels")
    var filled_total = 0
    var i = 0
    while i < n_ops:
        var t0 = perf_counter_ns()
        var filled = book.match_market(150)
        var dt = perf_counter_ns() - t0
        st.add(dt)
        filled_total += filled
        book.refill()
        i += 1
    assert_equal(filled_total, n_ops * 150)
    return st^


def bench_parse(n_ops: Int) raises -> LatencyStats:
    var msgs = List[String]()
    var expected = 0
    var i = 0
    while i < n_ops:
        var price = 10000 + (i % 50)
        var qty = 1 + (i % 9)
        expected += price + qty
        var msg = (
            String("T|")
            + String(i)
            + String("|1700000000|AAPL|B|")
            + String(price)
            + String("|")
            + String(qty)
        )
        msgs.append(msg)
        i += 1
    var st = LatencyStats("parse-tick")
    var total = 0
    var j = 0
    while j < n_ops:
        var t0 = perf_counter_ns()
        var p, q = parse_tick_fields(msgs[j])
        var dt = perf_counter_ns() - t0
        st.add(dt)
        total += p + q
        j += 1
    assert_equal(total, expected)
    return st^


def bench_risk(n_ops: Int) raises -> LatencyStats:
    seed(777)
    var prices = List[Int]()
    var qtys = List[Int]()
    var expected_ok = 0
    var i = 0
    while i < n_ops:
        var price = 9000 + Int(random_ui64(0, 2000))
        var qty = 1 + Int(random_ui64(0, 1200))
        prices.append(price)
        qtys.append(qty)
        if risk_check_inline(price, qty):
            expected_ok += 1
        i += 1
    var st = LatencyStats("risk-check")
    var ok_count = 0
    var j = 0
    while j < n_ops:
        var t0 = perf_counter_ns()
        var ok = risk_check_inline(prices[j], qtys[j])
        var dt = perf_counter_ns() - t0
        st.add(dt)
        if ok:
            ok_count += 1
        j += 1
    assert_equal(ok_count, expected_ok)
    return st^


def bench_empty(n_ops: Int) -> LatencyStats:
    var st = LatencyStats("empty-loop")
    var i = 0
    while i < n_ops:
        var t0 = perf_counter_ns()
        var dt = perf_counter_ns() - t0
        st.add(dt)
        i += 1
    return st^


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Tests. The timing assertions are structural (counts, ordering, totals);
# the algorithmic assertions are exact (comparison counts, percentiles of
# a known sample, histogram buckets of a known sample).
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def test_percentiles_known() raises:
    var st = LatencyStats("known")
    st.add(10)
    st.add(20)
    st.add(30)
    st.add(40)
    st.add(50)
    assert_equal(st.count(), 5)
    assert_almost_equal(st.mean_ns(), 30.0, atol=1e-9)
    assert_equal(st.min_ns(), 10)
    assert_equal(st.p50(), 30)
    assert_equal(st.p90(), 50)
    assert_equal(st.p99(), 50)
    assert_equal(st.p999(), 50)
    assert_equal(st.max_ns(), 50)
    assert_almost_equal(st.stddev_ns(), 14.142135623730951, atol=1e-6)
    assert_equal(st.total_ns(), 150)
    assert_almost_equal(st.ops_per_sec(), 33333333.333333336, atol=1.0)
    print("percentiles ok: known sample gives exact mean/p50/p99/stddev")


def test_histogram_known() raises:
    var st = LatencyStats("hist")
    st.add(5)
    st.add(15)
    st.add(25)
    st.add(35)
    st.add(45)
    var edges = List[Int]()
    edges.append(10)
    edges.append(20)
    edges.append(30)
    edges.append(40)
    var counts = st.histogram(edges)
    assert_equal(len(counts), 5)
    assert_equal(counts[0], 1)
    assert_equal(counts[1], 1)
    assert_equal(counts[2], 1)
    assert_equal(counts[3], 1)
    assert_equal(counts[4], 1)
    var total = 0
    var i = 0
    while i < len(counts):
        total += counts[i]
        i += 1
    assert_equal(total, 5)
    print("histogram ok: known sample lands one per bucket")


def test_find_counts() raises:
    var book = LevelBook()
    book.build(1000, 10000, 1, 10)
    var idx1, comps1 = book.find_linear(10999)
    assert_equal(idx1, 999)
    assert_equal(comps1, 1000)
    var idx2, comps2 = book.find_binary(10999)
    assert_equal(idx2, 999)
    assert_true(comps2 <= 10)
    assert_true(comps2 < comps1)
    var idx3, comps3 = book.find_linear(10000)
    assert_equal(idx3, 0)
    assert_equal(comps3, 1)
    var idx4, comps4 = book.find_binary(10000)
    assert_equal(idx4, 0)
    assert_true(comps4 <= 10)
    var idx5, comps5 = book.find_linear(20000)
    assert_equal(idx5, 1000)
    assert_equal(comps5, 1000)
    var idx6, comps6 = book.find_binary(20000)
    assert_equal(idx6, 1000)
    assert_true(comps6 <= 10)
    print("find counts ok: linear 1000 vs binary <=10 on a 1000-level book")


def test_match_correctness() raises:
    var book = LevelBook()
    book.build(5, 10000, 1, 10)
    assert_equal(book.total_qty(), 50)
    assert_equal(book.match_market(7), 7)
    assert_equal(book.total_qty(), 43)
    assert_equal(book.qty[0], 3)
    assert_equal(book.match_market(25), 25)
    assert_equal(book.total_qty(), 18)
    book.refill()
    assert_equal(book.total_qty(), 50)
    assert_equal(book.qty[0], 10)
    print("match correctness ok: partial fills, sweep, refill")


def test_insert_find() raises:
    var book = LevelBook()
    book.build(3, 10000, 1, 10)
    book.insert_linear(10001, 5)
    assert_equal(book.qty[1], 15)
    assert_equal(book.n_levels(), 3)
    book.insert_linear(9999, 4)
    assert_equal(book.n_levels(), 4)
    assert_equal(book.px[0], 9999)
    assert_equal(book.qty[0], 4)
    var book2 = LevelBook()
    book2.build(3, 10000, 1, 10)
    book2.insert_binary(10001, 5)
    assert_equal(book2.qty[1], 15)
    book2.insert_binary(10005, 2)
    assert_equal(book2.n_levels(), 4)
    assert_equal(book2.px[3], 10005)
    print("insert/find ok: merge at existing level, insert at both ends")


def test_benchmarks_small() raises:
    var n = 200
    var s1 = bench_find_linear(n, 200)
    assert_equal(s1.count(), n)
    assert_true(s1.min_ns() <= s1.p50())
    assert_true(s1.p50() <= s1.p90())
    assert_true(s1.p90() <= s1.p99())
    assert_true(s1.p99() <= s1.max_ns())
    assert_true(s1.mean_ns() >= Float64(s1.min_ns()))
    assert_true(s1.mean_ns() <= Float64(s1.max_ns()))
    assert_true(s1.stddev_ns() >= 0.0)
    assert_true(s1.ops_per_sec() > 0.0)
    var edges = List[Int]()
    edges.append(100)
    edges.append(500)
    edges.append(2000)
    var counts = s1.histogram(edges)
    var total = 0
    var i = 0
    while i < len(counts):
        total += counts[i]
        i += 1
    assert_equal(total, n)
    var s2 = bench_find_binary(n, 200)
    assert_equal(s2.count(), n)
    assert_true(s2.p50() <= s2.p99())
    var s3 = bench_match_top(n)
    assert_equal(s3.count(), n)
    var s4 = bench_parse(n)
    assert_equal(s4.count(), n)
    var s5 = bench_risk(n)
    assert_equal(s5.count(), n)
    print("benchmarks small ok: distributions are well-formed, fills exact")

    assert_equal(st.count(), 1000)
    assert_true(st.p50() < 100000)
    assert_true(st.min_ns() >= 0)
    print("empty overhead ok: measurement floor p50 =", st.p50(), "ns")

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Queue position: finding one order inside a price level.
#
# A matching engine constantly asks "where in the queue is order X?"
# (to cancel it, or to report its position). Two ways to answer: scan
# the level's order ids in order, or keep a map from order id to index.
# The scan is O(n) comparisons; the map is O(1). The comparison counts
# are deterministic and asserted; the timings show the constant-factor
# story on top. (A real engine keeps the map, and pays for it by
# updating the map on every insert and cancel -- there is no free lunch,
# only a choice of where to pay.)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def bench_queue_scan(n_ops: Int, n_orders: Int) raises -> LatencyStats:
    var ids = List[Int]()
    var i = 0
    while i < n_orders:
        ids.append(i)
        i += 1
    seed(4242)
    var targets = List[Int]()
    var expected = 0
    var j = 0
    while j < n_ops:
        var t = Int(random_ui64(0, UInt64(n_orders - 1)))
        targets.append(t)
        expected += t
        j += 1
    var st = LatencyStats("queue-scan")
    var found_total = 0
    var k = 0
    while k < n_ops:
        var target = targets[k]
        var t0 = perf_counter_ns()
        var idx = -1
        var m = 0
        while m < len(ids):
            if ids[m] == target:
                idx = m
                break
            m += 1
        var dt = perf_counter_ns() - t0
        st.add(dt)
        found_total += idx
        k += 1
    assert_equal(found_total, expected)
    return st^


def bench_queue_dict(n_ops: Int, n_orders: Int) raises -> LatencyStats:
    var index = Dict[Int, Int]()
    var i = 0
    while i < n_orders:
        index[i] = i
        i += 1
    seed(4242)
    var targets = List[Int]()
    var expected = 0
    var j = 0
    while j < n_ops:
        var t = Int(random_ui64(0, UInt64(n_orders - 1)))
        targets.append(t)
        expected += t
        j += 1
    var st = LatencyStats("queue-dict")
    var found_total = 0
    var k = 0
    while k < n_ops:
        var target = targets[k]
        var t0 = perf_counter_ns()
        var idx = index.get(target, -1)
        var dt = perf_counter_ns() - t0
        st.add(dt)
        found_total += idx
        k += 1
    assert_equal(found_total, expected)
    return st^


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Pipeline: the whole order path in one timed operation -- parse the
# wire message, run the risk arithmetic, match against the book, and
# write the fill report. The stage benchmarks above time each piece
# alone; this one times them together, which is the number a gateway
# actually has to meet. (Percentiles do not add, so the pipeline p50
# is not the sum of the stage p50s; the MEANS roughly are, and the
# demo prints both so the reader can see the difference.)
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def bench_pipeline(n_ops: Int) raises -> LatencyStats:
    var book = LevelBook()
    book.build(200, 10000, 1, 10)
    var msgs = List[String]()
    var expected = 0
    var i = 0
    while i < n_ops:
        var price = 10000 + (i % 50)
        var qty = 1 + (i % 9)
        expected += price + qty
        var msg = (
            String("T|")
            + String(i)
            + String("|1700000000|AAPL|B|")
            + String(price)
            + String("|")
            + String(qty)
        )
        msgs.append(msg)
        i += 1
    var st = LatencyStats("pipeline-parse-risk-match-report")
    var parsed_total = 0
    var filled_total = 0
    var report_px = List[Int]()
    var report_qty = List[Int]()
    var j = 0
    while j < n_ops:
        var t0 = perf_counter_ns()
        var p, q = parse_tick_fields(msgs[j])
        var ok = risk_check_inline(p, q)
        var filled = 0
        if ok:
            filled = book.match_market(q)
            report_px.append(p)
            report_qty.append(filled)
        var dt = perf_counter_ns() - t0
        st.add(dt)
        parsed_total += p + q
        filled_total += filled
        book.qty[0] += filled
        j += 1
    assert_equal(parsed_total, expected)
    assert_equal(len(report_px), n_ops)
    return st^


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Stability: the same benchmark three times. A benchmark that cannot
# reproduce its own numbers is not measuring the code, it is measuring
# the weather. The p50s of three identical runs should cluster; if
# they do not, the machine was busy and the numbers should be
# re-run, not believed.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def bench_stability(n_ops: Int) raises -> List[Int]:
    var p50s = List[Int]()
    var r = 0
    while r < 3:
        var st = bench_match_top(n_ops)
        p50s.append(st.p50())
        r += 1
    return p50s^


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Extra tests.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def test_queue_position_counts() raises:
    var ids = List[Int]()
    var i = 0
    while i < 1000:
        ids.append(i)
        i += 1
    # Linear scan for the last id costs 1000 comparisons; a dict costs 1.
    var comps = 0
    var idx = -1
    var j = 0
    while j < len(ids):
        comps += 1
        if ids[j] == 999:
            idx = j
            break
        j += 1
    assert_equal(idx, 999)
    assert_equal(comps, 1000)
    var index = Dict[Int, Int]()
    var k = 0
    while k < 1000:
        index[k] = k
        k += 1
    assert_equal(index.get(999, -1), 999)
    assert_equal(index.get(12345, -1), -1)
    print("queue position ok: scan 1000 comparisons, dict 1 lookup")


def test_pipeline_small() raises:
    var st = bench_pipeline(200)
    assert_equal(st.count(), 200)
    assert_true(st.min_ns() <= st.p50())
    assert_true(st.p50() <= st.p99())
    assert_true(st.ops_per_sec() > 0.0)
    var s1 = bench_queue_scan(200, 200)
    var s2 = bench_queue_dict(200, 200)
    assert_equal(s1.count(), 200)
    assert_equal(s2.count(), 200)
    print("pipeline small ok: full path timed, queue scan vs dict timed")


def test_stability_small() raises:
    var p50s = bench_stability(200)
    assert_equal(len(p50s), 3)
    var lo = p50s[0]
    var hi = p50s[0]
    var i = 0
    while i < 3:
        if p50s[i] < lo:
            lo = p50s[i]
        if p50s[i] > hi:
            hi = p50s[i]
        i += 1
    assert_true(lo > 0)
    assert_true(hi <= lo * 10)
    print("stability ok: three runs, p50s within 10x of each other")

--- SOURCE: mojo_samples/hft_market_maker.mojo ---
# hft_market_maker.mojo
#
# Original code written for the START project's Mojo code samples.
# Component 3 (HFT FRAMEWORKS): a market-making quoting engine.
#
# SIMULATOR ONLY. This file simulates a market maker quoting against an
# invented price path inside this program. It never connects to an
# exchange, a broker, or any market-data vendor, and no real money is
# involved anywhere.
#
# Standard library only. No external packages, no Python interop, no network.
#
# What a market maker does: it always shows a price to buy at (the bid)
# and a price to sell at (the ask), and earns the spread between them.
# The naive version of this loses money, because the market does not buy
# and sell at random: it buys from the maker when the price is about to
# rise (so the maker sells cheap) and sells to the maker when the price
# is about to fall (so the maker buys dear). That is adverse selection,
# and surviving it is the whole job. This file implements the three
# standard defences, each testable in isolation:
#
#   1. Fair value from the microprice. The mid-price (bid+ask)/2 treats
#      both sides equally, but the book is rarely balanced. The
#      microprice weights each side by the OTHER side's size:
#        microprice = (bid * ask_qty + ask * bid_qty) / (bid_qty + ask_qty)
#      When the bid is much larger than the ask, the microprice sits
#      above the mid: the weight of money says the next move is up.
#      Hand check: bid 10000 x30, ask 10100 x10 ->
#      (10000*10 + 10100*30) / 40 = 403000/40 = 10075.
#      Quoting around the microprice instead of the mid means the
#      maker's quotes already lean the way the book leans.
#
#   2. Inventory skew. A market maker that is long 50 units does not
#      want to buy 10 more; it wants to sell. So it shifts BOTH quotes
#      down by a skew that grows with inventory: lower ask (easier for
#      others to buy from it) and lower bid (harder for others to sell
#      to it). A short maker shifts both quotes up. The skew pulls
#      inventory back towards zero, which is the maker's risk control:
#      inventory is risk, and the quotes are the tool that manages it.
#      The size skew does the same job on quantities: a long maker
#      shows a smaller bid and a larger ask.
#
#   3. Spread widening with inventory. The further inventory is from
#      zero, the wider the quotes: a wide quote is a reluctant quote.
#      At the inventory limit the maker stops quoting on the side that
#      would make inventory worse (a hard stop, not a suggestion).
#
# The simulation in this file replays a price path bar by bar. At each
# bar the maker refreshes its quotes around the new fair value, then the
# bar's trade is applied: a trade at or below the maker's bid fills the
# bid (the maker buys), a trade at or above the maker's ask fills the
# ask (the maker sells). Fills update inventory and cash. Two PnL views
# are kept, because a market maker needs both:
#   * Mark-to-market equity: cash + inventory * current mark. This is
#     what the maker is worth right now.
#   * Adverse-selection accounting: every fill is marked again a few
#     bars later. A fill that looked like spread capture at the time
#     but loses money five bars later was adverse selection, and the
#     difference between "edge at fill" and "edge after five bars" is
#     the maker's most honest performance number.
#
# The file also runs the same path through a naive maker (fixed spread,
# no skew, no size adjustment, no inventory limit) so the reader can
# see what the three defences are worth. On a trending path the naive
# maker accumulates a large inventory against the trend and loses; the
# skewing maker stays near flat and keeps the spread. The demo prints
# both; the tests assert the structural guarantees (inventory limits,
# accounting identities, quote arithmetic) exactly, and assert the
# comparison only where the test path makes it a theorem rather than
# a hope (the trend path below is constructed so the naive maker must
# accumulate inventory: every bar trades above its ask).
#
#   4. Jump guard (volatility guard). When the fair value jumps by
#      more than `jump_threshold` ticks between refreshes, the maker
#      widens its quotes by `jump_widen` ticks for that refresh. A
#      fast market is an uncertain market, and a quote placed on stale
#      information is the one that gets picked off. The guard is the
#      maker's way of saying "I do not trust this price yet".
#
# A note on what is NOT simulated: queue position (in reality a quote
# only fills if it is at the front of its price level when the trade
# arrives), latency (in reality a quote can be stale when it is hit),
# and fees/rebates (in reality the exchange pays the maker a small
# rebate per fill). Each would change the numbers; none changes the
# structure, and the file says where each would plug in.

from std.testing import assert_equal, assert_true, assert_almost_equal
from std.math import sqrt

comptime SIDE_BUY = 0
comptime SIDE_SELL = 1


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Microprice: the size-weighted fair value of a two-sided book.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def microprice(bid: Int, bid_qty: Int, ask: Int, ask_qty: Int) -> Float64:
    var total = bid_qty + ask_qty
    if total <= 0:
        return (Float64(bid) + Float64(ask)) / 2.0
    var num = Float64(bid) * Float64(ask_qty) + Float64(ask) * Float64(bid_qty)
    return num / Float64(total)


def mid_price(bid: Int, ask: Int) -> Float64:
    return (Float64(bid) + Float64(ask)) / 2.0


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# QuoteParams: the maker's policy, in ticks and units.
#
#   base_half_spread  half the quoted spread at zero inventory.
#   base_size         units shown per level at zero inventory.
#   n_levels          levels quoted per side (ladder: half-spread
#                     grows by one base_half_spread per level out).
#   skew_num/skew_den skew ticks = inventory * skew_num // skew_den.
#   size_step         inventory units per one unit of size skew.
#   widen_ticks        extra half-spread ticks at maximum inventory.
#   max_inventory     hard cap on |inventory|.
#   jump_threshold    fair-value move (ticks) that triggers the guard.
#   jump_widen        extra half-spread ticks while the guard is on.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

struct QuoteParams:
    var base_half_spread: Int
    var base_size: Int
    var n_levels: Int
    var skew_num: Int
    var skew_den: Int
    var size_step: Int
    var widen_ticks: Int
    var max_inventory: Int
    var jump_threshold: Int
    var jump_widen: Int

    def __init__(out self):
        self.base_half_spread = 10
        self.base_size = 10
        self.n_levels = 3
        self.skew_num = 1
        self.skew_den = 10
        self.size_step = 5
        self.widen_ticks = 5
        self.max_inventory = 100
        self.jump_threshold = 20
        self.jump_widen = 15

    def describe(self) -> String:
        var s = String("params: half_spread=") + String(self.base_half_spread)
        s = s + String(" size=") + String(self.base_size)
        s = s + String(" levels=") + String(self.n_levels)
        s = s + String(" skew=") + String(self.skew_num) + String("/")
        s = s + String(self.skew_den) + String(" widen=") + String(self.widen_ticks)
        s = s + String(" max_inv=") + String(self.max_inventory)
        s = s + String(" jump=") + String(self.jump_threshold)
        s = s + String("+") + String(self.jump_widen)
        return s


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Quote: one live order the maker is showing. quote_id is assigned by
# the maker; ts is the bar index when it was placed (its age).
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

struct Quote:
    var quote_id: Int
    var side: Int
    var price: Int
    var qty: Int
    var level: Int
    var placed_bar: Int

    def __init__(
        out self, quote_id: Int, side: Int, price: Int, qty: Int, level: Int, placed_bar: Int
    ):
        self.quote_id = quote_id
        self.side = side
        self.price = price
        self.qty = qty
        self.level = level
        self.placed_bar = placed_bar

    def describe(self) -> String:
        var side_s = String("ASK")
        if self.side == SIDE_BUY:
            side_s = String("BID")
        return (
            String("q")
            + String(self.quote_id)
            + String(" ")
            + side_s
            + String(" L")
            + String(self.level)
            + String(" px=")
            + String(self.price)
            + String(" qty=")
            + String(self.qty)
        )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Quote computation (pure functions, so the tests can check the arithmetic
# without running a simulation).
#
#   half_spread(inventory): base half-spread plus widening that grows
#     linearly with |inventory|/max_inventory.
#   skew_ticks(inventory): signed shift applied to BOTH quotes. Positive
#     inventory (long) shifts quotes down; negative shifts them up.
#   bid_size / ask_size: size skew, clamped to [0, 3 * base_size].
#   compute_quotes: the full ladder. Returns (bid prices, bid sizes,
#     ask prices, ask sizes) as four parallel lists, best level first.
#     A side with size 0 at a level is skipped (no quote there).
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def half_spread(params: QuoteParams, inventory: Int) -> Int:
    var abs_inv = inventory
    if abs_inv < 0:
        abs_inv = -abs_inv
    if abs_inv > params.max_inventory:
        abs_inv = params.max_inventory
    return params.base_half_spread + (abs_inv * params.widen_ticks) // params.max_inventory


def skew_ticks(params: QuoteParams, inventory: Int) -> Int:
    return inventory * params.skew_num // params.skew_den


def bid_size(params: QuoteParams, inventory: Int) -> Int:
    var s = params.base_size - inventory // params.size_step
    if s < 0:
        s = 0
    var cap = 3 * params.base_size
    if s > cap:
        s = cap
    return s


def ask_size(params: QuoteParams, inventory: Int) -> Int:
    var s = params.base_size + inventory // params.size_step
    if s < 0:
        s = 0
    var cap = 3 * params.base_size
    if s > cap:
        s = cap
    return s


struct QuoteLadder:
    var bid_px: List[Int]
    var bid_qty: List[Int]
    var ask_px: List[Int]
    var ask_qty: List[Int]

    def __init__(out self):
        self.bid_px = List[Int]()
        self.bid_qty = List[Int]()
        self.ask_px = List[Int]()
        self.ask_qty = List[Int]()


def compute_quotes(
    params: QuoteParams, fair: Float64, inventory: Int, extra_half: Int = 0
) -> QuoteLadder:
    var ladder = QuoteLadder()
    var h = half_spread(params, inventory) + extra_half
    var sk = skew_ticks(params, inventory)
    var centre = Int(fair)
    var bs = bid_size(params, inventory)
    var asize = ask_size(params, inventory)
    var level = 0
    while level < params.n_levels:
        var half = h + level * params.base_half_spread
        if bs > 0:
            ladder.bid_px.append(centre - half - sk)
            ladder.bid_qty.append(bs)
        if asize > 0:
            ladder.ask_px.append(centre + half - sk)
            ladder.ask_qty.append(asize)
        level += 1
    return ladder^

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# MakerFill: one fill of a maker quote, with the fair value at the time
# (for edge accounting) and the bar index (for later marking).
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

struct MakerFill:
    var bar_idx: Int
    var side: Int
    var price: Int
    var qty: Int
    var fair_at_fill: Float64

    def __init__(out self, bar_idx: Int, side: Int, price: Int, qty: Int, fair_at_fill: Float64):
        self.bar_idx = bar_idx
        self.side = side
        self.price = price
        self.qty = qty
        self.fair_at_fill = fair_at_fill

    # Edge captured at the moment of the fill: how far inside the fair
    # value the trade happened. Positive means the maker bought below
    # fair value or sold above it.
    def edge_ticks(self) -> Float64:
        if self.side == SIDE_BUY:
            return (self.fair_at_fill - Float64(self.price)) * Float64(self.qty)
        return (Float64(self.price) - self.fair_at_fill) * Float64(self.qty)


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# MarketMaker: inventory, cash, live quotes, and statistics.
#
# The maker is deliberately simple about the market: the simulation
# hands it a fair value and a trade price each bar. The maker refreshes
# its quotes around the fair value, then any trade at or through a
# quote fills it. A real maker would also manage queue position and
# latency; the hooks for those are the quote age (placed_bar) and the
# refresh cadence (one refresh per bar here).
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

struct MarketMaker:
    var params: QuoteParams
    var use_skew: Bool
    var inventory: Int
    var cash: Int
    var quotes: List[Quote]
    var fills: List[MakerFill]
    var next_quote_id: Int
    var n_refreshes: Int
    var n_fills: Int
    var volume_traded: Int
    var last_fair: Float64
    var last_mark: Int
    var marks: List[Int]

    def __init__(out self, use_skew: Bool):
        self.params = QuoteParams()
        self.use_skew = use_skew
        self.inventory = 0
        self.cash = 0
        self.quotes = List[Quote]()
        self.fills = List[MakerFill]()
        self.next_quote_id = 1
        self.n_refreshes = 0
        self.n_fills = 0
        self.volume_traded = 0
        self.last_fair = 0.0
        self.last_mark = 0
        self.marks = List[Int]()

    def set_params(mut self, var p: QuoteParams):
        self.params = p^

    def equity(self) -> Int:
        return self.cash + self.inventory * self.last_mark

    def abs_inventory(self) -> Int:
        if self.inventory < 0:
            return -self.inventory
        return self.inventory

    # Cancel every live quote.
    def cancel_all(mut self) -> Int:
        var n = len(self.quotes)
        self.quotes = List[Quote]()
        return n

    # Refresh quotes around a new fair value: cancel everything, then
    # place the new ladder. A side that would increase inventory beyond
    # the cap is not quoted at all (the hard stop).
    def refresh(mut self, fair: Float64, bar_idx: Int):
        var extra_half = 0
        if self.last_fair != 0.0:
            var move = fair - self.last_fair
            if move < 0.0:
                move = -move
            if Int(move) > self.params.jump_threshold:
                extra_half = self.params.jump_widen
        self.last_fair = fair
        var _cancelled = self.cancel_all()
        self.n_refreshes += 1
        var inv = self.inventory
        if not self.use_skew:
            inv = 0
        var ladder = compute_quotes(self.params, fair, inv, extra_half)
        var i = 0
        while i < len(ladder.bid_px):
            if self.inventory < self.params.max_inventory:
                var q = Quote(
                    self.next_quote_id, SIDE_BUY, ladder.bid_px[i], ladder.bid_qty[i], i, bar_idx
                )
                self.next_quote_id += 1
                self.quotes.append(q^)
            i += 1
        var j = 0
        while j < len(ladder.ask_px):
            if self.inventory > -self.params.max_inventory:
                var q = Quote(
                    self.next_quote_id, SIDE_SELL, ladder.ask_px[j], ladder.ask_qty[j], j, bar_idx
                )
                self.next_quote_id += 1
                self.quotes.append(q^)
            j += 1

    def best_bid(self) -> Int:
        var best = -1
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.quotes):
            if self.quotes[i].side == SIDE_BUY:
                if best < 0 or self.quotes[i].price > best:
                    best = self.quotes[i].price
            i += 1
        return best

    def best_ask(self) -> Int:
        var best = -1
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.quotes):
            if self.quotes[i].side == SIDE_SELL:
                if best < 0 or self.quotes[i].price < best:
                    best = self.quotes[i].price
            i += 1
        return best

    def bid_qty_at_best(self) -> Int:
        var best = self.best_bid()
        var total = 0
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.quotes):
            if self.quotes[i].side == SIDE_BUY and self.quotes[i].price == best:
                total += self.quotes[i].qty
            i += 1
        return total

    def ask_qty_at_best(self) -> Int:
        var best = self.best_ask()
        var total = 0
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.quotes):
            if self.quotes[i].side == SIDE_SELL and self.quotes[i].price == best:
                total += self.quotes[i].qty
            i += 1
        return total

    # Apply one market trade. A trade priced at or below the best bid
    # fills the maker's bid (the maker buys); at or above the best ask
    # it fills the ask (the maker sells). Fills consume the best level
    # first, then deeper levels. Returns the filled quantity.
    def on_trade(mut self, price: Int, qty: Int, bar_idx: Int) -> Int:
        var filled = 0
        var remaining = qty
        # Sell trades hitting the bid: the maker buys.
        if len(self.quotes) > 0 and price <= self.best_bid():
            while remaining > 0:
                var idx = -1
                var best = -1
                var i = 0
                while i < len(self.quotes):
                    if self.quotes[i].side == SIDE_BUY:
                        if best < 0 or self.quotes[i].price > best:
                            best = self.quotes[i].price
                            idx = i
                    i += 1
                if idx < 0:
                    break
                var take = remaining
                if self.quotes[idx].qty < take:
                    take = self.quotes[idx].qty
                var px = self.quotes[idx].price
                self.quotes[idx].qty -= take
                if self.quotes[idx].qty == 0:
                    var _gone = self.quotes.pop(idx)
                self.inventory += take
                self.cash -= px * take
                self.n_fills += 1
                self.volume_traded += take
                filled += take
                remaining -= take
                var f = MakerFill(bar_idx, SIDE_BUY, px, take, self.last_fair)
                self.fills.append(f^)
                if remaining > 0 and (len(self.quotes) == 0 or price > self.best_bid()):
                    break
        # Buy trades hitting the ask: the maker sells.
        if len(self.quotes) > 0 and price >= self.best_ask():
            while remaining > 0:
                var idx = -1
                var best = -1
                var i = 0
                while i < len(self.quotes):
                    if self.quotes[i].side == SIDE_SELL:
                        if best < 0 or self.quotes[i].price < best:
                            best = self.quotes[i].price
                            idx = i
                    i += 1
                if idx < 0:
                    break
                var take = remaining
                if self.quotes[idx].qty < take:
                    take = self.quotes[idx].qty
                var px = self.quotes[idx].price
                self.quotes[idx].qty -= take
                if self.quotes[idx].qty == 0:
                    var _gone = self.quotes.pop(idx)
                self.inventory -= take
                self.cash += px * take
                self.n_fills += 1
                self.volume_traded += take
                filled += take
                remaining -= take
                var f = MakerFill(bar_idx, SIDE_SELL, px, take, self.last_fair)
                self.fills.append(f^)
                if remaining > 0 and (len(self.quotes) == 0 or price < self.best_ask()):
                    break
        return filled

    # Total edge captured at fill time, summed over all fills.
    def total_edge(self) -> Float64:
        var total: Float64 = 0.0
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.fills):
            total += self.fills[i].edge_ticks()
            i += 1
        return total

    # Adverse-selection accounting: re-mark every fill at the mark
    # `lag` bars after it filled. A buy that the market then trades
    # below was adverse; a sell the market then trades above was too.
    # Returns the summed later PnL of all fills old enough to mark.
    def later_pnl(self, lag: Int) -> Float64:
        var total: Float64 = 0.0
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.fills):
            var mark_idx = self.fills[i].bar_idx + lag
            if mark_idx < len(self.marks):
                var mark = Float64(self.marks[mark_idx])
                if self.fills[i].side == SIDE_BUY:
                    total += (mark - Float64(self.fills[i].price)) * Float64(self.fills[i].qty)
                else:
                    total += (Float64(self.fills[i].price) - mark) * Float64(self.fills[i].qty)
            i += 1
        return total

    def describe(self) -> String:
        var s = String("maker: inv=") + String(self.inventory)
        s = s + String(" cash=") + String(self.cash)
        s = s + String(" equity=") + String(self.equity())
        s = s + String(" fills=") + String(self.n_fills)
        s = s + String(" volume=") + String(self.volume_traded)
        s = s + String(" edge=") + String(self.total_edge())
        return s


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Simulation: replay a mark path through a maker.
#
# Each bar: the maker refreshes quotes around the current mark (the book
# is synthesised as mark - 5 / mark + 5 with size 20/20, so the
# microprice equals the mark and the test path is neutral about the
# book), then the mark moves by `drift_per_bar` and a trade prints at
# the NEW mark. When the mark rises through the maker's ask the ask is
# lifted (the maker sells); when it falls through the bid the bid is
# hit (the maker buys). With a drift of +10 and a base half-spread of
# 10, the new mark lands exactly on the best ask every bar, so a naive
# maker sells 10 units a bar until its inventory cap stops it, while a
# skewing maker's ask has moved one tick higher after the first sale
# and is never hit again. That is the comparison the tests assert.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def run_maker_sim(
    use_skew: Bool, start_mark: Int, n_bars: Int, drift_per_bar: Int, trade_qty: Int
) -> MarketMaker:
    var maker = MarketMaker(use_skew)
    var mark = start_mark
    var bar = 0
    while bar < n_bars:
        maker.marks.append(mark)
        maker.last_mark = mark
        var fair = microprice(mark - 5, 20, mark + 5, 20)
        maker.refresh(fair, bar)
        mark += drift_per_bar
        var _filled = maker.on_trade(mark, trade_qty, bar)
        maker.last_mark = mark
        bar += 1
    return maker^

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Tests.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def test_microprice() raises:
    # (10000*10 + 10100*30) / 40 = 403000/40 = 10075.
    assert_almost_equal(microprice(10000, 30, 10100, 10), 10075.0, atol=1e-9)
    assert_almost_equal(microprice(10000, 10, 10100, 10), 10050.0, atol=1e-9)
    assert_almost_equal(microprice(10000, 0, 10100, 0), 10050.0, atol=1e-9)
    assert_almost_equal(mid_price(10000, 10100), 10050.0, atol=1e-9)
    print("microprice ok: size-weighted fair value, balanced case is the mid")


def test_spread_skew_sizes() raises:
    var p = QuoteParams()
    assert_equal(half_spread(p, 0), 10)
    assert_equal(half_spread(p, 100), 15)
    assert_equal(half_spread(p, -100), 15)
    assert_equal(half_spread(p, 50), 12)
    assert_equal(skew_ticks(p, 50), 5)
    assert_equal(skew_ticks(p, -30), -3)
    assert_equal(skew_ticks(p, 0), 0)
    assert_equal(bid_size(p, 0), 10)
    assert_equal(ask_size(p, 0), 10)
    assert_equal(bid_size(p, 50), 0)
    assert_equal(ask_size(p, 50), 20)
    assert_equal(bid_size(p, -30), 16)
    assert_equal(ask_size(p, -30), 4)
    assert_equal(bid_size(p, 200), 0)
    assert_equal(ask_size(p, 200), 30)
    print("spread/skew/sizes ok: widening, skew direction, size clamps")


def test_compute_quotes_flat() raises:
    var p = QuoteParams()
    var q = compute_quotes(p, 10050.0, 0)
    assert_equal(len(q.bid_px), 3)
    assert_equal(q.bid_px[0], 10040)
    assert_equal(q.bid_px[1], 10030)
    assert_equal(q.bid_px[2], 10020)
    assert_equal(q.bid_qty[0], 10)
    assert_equal(len(q.ask_px), 3)
    assert_equal(q.ask_px[0], 10060)
    assert_equal(q.ask_px[1], 10070)
    assert_equal(q.ask_px[2], 10080)
    assert_equal(q.ask_qty[2], 10)
    print("quotes flat ok: ladder 10040/10060, 10030/10070, 10020/10080")


def test_compute_quotes_skewed() raises:
    var p = QuoteParams()
    # Long 50: half-spread 12, skew 5 down, bid size 0, ask size 20.
    var q = compute_quotes(p, 10050.0, 50)
    assert_equal(len(q.bid_px), 0)
    assert_equal(len(q.ask_px), 3)
    assert_equal(q.ask_px[0], 10057)
    assert_equal(q.ask_px[1], 10067)
    assert_equal(q.ask_px[2], 10077)
    assert_equal(q.ask_qty[0], 20)
    # Short 30: half-spread 11, skew 3 up, bid size 16, ask size 4.
    var r = compute_quotes(p, 10050.0, -30)
    assert_equal(r.bid_px[0], 10042)
    assert_equal(r.ask_px[0], 10064)
    assert_equal(r.bid_qty[0], 16)
    assert_equal(r.ask_qty[0], 4)
    print("quotes skewed ok: long maker drops bids, short maker lifts asks")


def test_refresh_and_fill() raises:
    var maker = MarketMaker(True)
    maker.refresh(10050.0, 0)
    assert_equal(maker.best_bid(), 10040)
    assert_equal(maker.best_ask(), 10060)
    assert_equal(len(maker.quotes), 6)
    var f1 = maker.on_trade(10040, 5, 0)
    assert_equal(f1, 5)
    assert_equal(maker.inventory, 5)
    assert_equal(maker.cash, -50200)
    assert_equal(maker.bid_qty_at_best(), 5)
    var f2 = maker.on_trade(10060, 4, 0)
    assert_equal(f2, 4)
    assert_equal(maker.inventory, 1)
    assert_equal(maker.cash, -9960)
    assert_equal(maker.n_fills, 2)
    assert_equal(maker.volume_traded, 9)
    # A trade inside the spread touches nothing.
    var f3 = maker.on_trade(10050, 100, 0)
    assert_equal(f3, 0)
    assert_equal(maker.inventory, 1)
    print("refresh/fill ok: fills consume the best level, spread is safe")


def test_jump_guard() raises:
    var maker = MarketMaker(True)
    # First refresh: no previous fair value, no widening.
    maker.refresh(10050.0, 0)
    assert_equal(maker.best_bid(), 10040)
    assert_equal(maker.best_ask(), 10060)
    # A 30-tick jump exceeds the threshold of 20: half-spread grows
    # from 10 to 25 for this refresh only.
    maker.refresh(10080.0, 1)
    assert_equal(maker.best_bid(), 10055)
    assert_equal(maker.best_ask(), 10105)
    # A 5-tick move does not trigger the guard: spread back to normal.
    maker.refresh(10085.0, 2)
    assert_equal(maker.best_bid(), 10075)
    assert_equal(maker.best_ask(), 10095)
    # Exactly at the threshold (20) does not trigger it either: the
    # guard needs a move strictly greater than the threshold.
    maker.refresh(10105.0, 3)
    assert_equal(maker.best_bid(), 10095)
    assert_equal(maker.best_ask(), 10115)
    print("jump guard ok: wide quotes after a jump, normal after calm")


def test_hard_stop() raises:
    var maker = MarketMaker(True)
    var p = QuoteParams()
    p.max_inventory = 10
    maker.set_params(p^)
    maker.refresh(10050.0, 0)
    var f = maker.on_trade(10060, 10, 0)
    assert_equal(f, 10)
    assert_equal(maker.inventory, -10)
    maker.refresh(10050.0, 1)
    # At the short cap the ask side is not quoted at all; the bid side
    # is still there, skewed up by 1 tick with a widened spread.
    assert_equal(maker.best_ask(), -1)
    assert_equal(maker.best_bid(), 10036)
    assert_true(maker.bid_qty_at_best() > 0)
    print("hard stop ok: capped side stops quoting, the other side continues")


def test_adverse_selection() raises:
    var maker = MarketMaker(True)
    maker.refresh(10050.0, 0)
    var f = maker.on_trade(10040, 10, 0)
    assert_equal(f, 10)
    # Edge at fill: bought 10 ticks below fair value -> +100.
    assert_almost_equal(maker.total_edge(), 100.0, atol=1e-9)
    maker.marks.append(10050)
    maker.marks.append(10030)
    maker.marks.append(10000)
    # Two bars later the mark is 10000: the fill is worth -400.
    assert_almost_equal(maker.later_pnl(2), -400.0, atol=1e-9)
    maker.last_mark = 10000
    assert_equal(maker.equity(), -400)
    print("adverse selection ok: edge +100 at fill, -400 two bars later")


def test_sim_uptrend() raises:
    var smart = run_maker_sim(True, 10000, 30, 10, 25)
    var naive = run_maker_sim(False, 10000, 30, 10, 25)
    # The naive maker sells 10 a bar until its cap: inventory -100.
    assert_equal(naive.inventory, -100)
    assert_equal(naive.n_fills, 10)
    assert_equal(naive.volume_traded, 100)
    assert_equal(naive.equity(), -24500)
    # The skewing maker sells once and is then never hit: inventory -10.
    assert_equal(smart.inventory, -10)
    assert_equal(smart.n_fills, 1)
    assert_equal(smart.volume_traded, 10)
    assert_equal(smart.equity(), -2900)
    assert_true(smart.equity() > naive.equity())
    assert_true(smart.abs_inventory() < naive.abs_inventory())
    # Accounting identity: cash + inventory * mark == equity.
    assert_equal(smart.cash + smart.inventory * smart.last_mark, smart.equity())
    assert_equal(naive.cash + naive.inventory * naive.last_mark, naive.equity())
    print("sim uptrend ok: naive -24500 at -100, smart -2900 at -10")


def test_sim_downtrend() raises:
    var smart = run_maker_sim(True, 10000, 30, -10, 25)
    var naive = run_maker_sim(False, 10000, 30, -10, 25)
    assert_equal(naive.inventory, 100)
    assert_equal(naive.equity(), -24500)
    assert_equal(smart.inventory, 10)
    assert_equal(smart.equity(), -2900)
    assert_true(smart.equity() > naive.equity())
    print("sim downtrend ok: mirror image, naive -24500, smart -2900")


def test_sim_flat() raises:
    var smart = run_maker_sim(True, 10000, 30, 0, 25)
    var naive = run_maker_sim(False, 10000, 30, 0, 25)
    assert_equal(smart.inventory, 0)
    assert_equal(smart.equity(), 0)
    assert_equal(smart.n_fills, 0)
    assert_equal(naive.inventory, 0)
    assert_equal(naive.equity(), 0)
    print("sim flat ok: no drift, no fills, no PnL")


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Demo: the three regimes, both makers.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

def demo() raises:
    print("=== HFT market maker demo (simulated data, no real market) ===")
    var p = QuoteParams()
    print(p.describe())
    var maker = MarketMaker(True)
    maker.refresh(10050.0, 0)
    print("ladder around fair 10050, flat inventory:")
    var i = 0
    while i < len(maker.quotes):
        print("  " + maker.quotes[i].describe())
        i += 1
    var regimes = List[Int]()
    regimes.append(10)
    regimes.append(0)
    regimes.append(-10)
    var r = 0
    while r < len(regimes):
        var drift = regimes[r]
        var smart = run_maker_sim(True, 10000, 30, drift, 25)
        var naive = run_maker_sim(False, 10000, 30, drift, 25)
        print("drift", drift, "per bar, 30 bars:")
        print("  smart: " + smart.describe())
        print("  naive: " + naive.describe())
        print("  smart edge at fill:", smart.total_edge(), " later PnL(5):", smart.later_pnl(5))
        print("  naive edge at fill:", naive.total_edge(), " later PnL(5):", naive.later_pnl(5))
        r += 1


def main() raises:
    demo()
    print("=== market maker tests ===")
    test_microprice()
    test_spread_skew_sizes()
    test_compute_quotes_flat()
    test_compute_quotes_skewed()
    test_refresh_and_fill()
    test_jump_guard()
    test_hard_stop()
    test_adverse_selection()
    test_sim_uptrend()
    test_sim_downtrend()
    test_sim_flat()
    print("ALL MARKET MAKER TESTS PASSED")

--- SOURCE: mojo_samples/hft_matching_engine.mojo ---
# hft_matching_engine.mojo
#
# Original code written for the START project's Mojo code samples.
# Component 3 (HFT FRAMEWORKS): a matching engine driving an order book.
#
# SIMULATOR ONLY. This engine matches invented orders against an invented
# book inside this program. It never connects to an exchange, a broker, or
# any market-data vendor, and no real money moves. Every order, trade, and
# price below is simulated data.
#
# Standard library only. No external packages, no Python interop, no network.
#
# What the engine does, in the order a real venue would:
#   1. Accepts orders (new / cancel / amend) and gives each a lifecycle:
#      NEW -> ACKED -> PARTIAL -> FILLED, or CANCELLED, or REJECTED.
#      A resting order is ACKED; once it has traded at all it is PARTIAL
#      until its remaining quantity reaches zero, when it becomes FILLED.
#   2. Runs a continuous book (limit / market / IOC / FOK) with price-time
#      priority, exactly like the order book in hft_order_book.mojo, but
#      with the engine around it: session phases, self-trade prevention,
#      trade reporting, and per-client positions.
#   3. Runs an opening auction: while the session is PREOPEN, orders are
#      collected but not matched. At the open the engine picks the clearing
#      price that maximises traded volume (ties break towards the reference
#      price, then towards the higher price), crosses everything it can at
#      that one price, and carries the unfilled remainder into the
#      continuous book.
#   4. Enforces session phases: PREOPEN collects for auction, OPEN matches
#      continuously, HALTED rejects new orders (cancels still work, so a
#      client can always reduce risk), CLOSED rejects everything.
#   5. Prevents self-trading: when an incoming order would trade against
#      the same client's resting order, the engine either cancels the
#      resting order (CANCEL_RESTING) or rejects the incoming order
#      (REJECT_INCOMING), depending on its configured mode.
#   6. Tracks per-client positions (quantity and cash, in ticks) from the
#      trade tape, so a strategy can always ask "what do I own right now".
#   7. Publishes a drop copy: every trade produces a TradeReport, an
#      immutable record of the fill with both order ids, both clients,
#      price, quantity, and whether it was an auction cross. The drop
#      copy is the venue's audit trail and the client's reconciliation
#      source: a client that disagrees with the venue about a fill
#      compares its own records against the drop copy. Reports are
#      append-only and numbered in order, so a missing report number
#      means a lost message, not a missing trade.
#
# Design notes:
#   * Prices are integer ticks (1 tick = 1 cent); quantities are whole
#     units. Matching arithmetic is exact.
#   * Orders are move-only structs, so an order lives in exactly one list
#     at a time: the auction lists, the continuous book, or the done list.
#     A trade never copies an order; it reads the fields it needs and the
#     book updates the resting order in place.
#   * The trade tape is the source of truth for positions. Position cash
#     and quantity are recomputed from the tape by the tests as a cross-
#     check, so a bookkeeping bug cannot hide.
#   * Validation happens before matching: a limit order needs a positive
#     price and quantity, a market order needs a positive quantity, and
#     an unknown order type is rejected. Rejections are logged with a
#     human-readable reason.
#
# Hand-computed auction case used in the tests:
#   Bids: 10100 x5, 10000 x10, 9900 x5.  Asks: 9900 x8, 10000 x6, 10100 x4.
#   Clearing-price volumes:
#     at  9900: bids >= 9900 hold 20, asks <= 9900 hold 8  -> volume 8
#     at 10000: bids >= 10000 hold 15, asks <= 10000 hold 14 -> volume 14
#     at 10100: bids >= 10100 hold 5,  asks <= 10100 hold 18 -> volume 5
#   Clearing price is 10000, volume 14. Fills at 10000: the 10100 bid
#   fills 5, the 10000 bid fills 9 of 10; the 9900 ask fills 8, the
#   10000 ask fills 6. Left over into the continuous book: bid 10000 x1,
#   bid 9900 x5, ask 10100 x4.
#
# Hand-computed position case used in the tests:
#   Client 1 buys 5 @10100 from client 2 (one trade). Afterwards:
#   client 1: qty +5, cash -50500.  client 2: qty -5, cash +50500.

from std.testing import assert_equal, assert_true, assert_almost_equal

# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Constants.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

comptime SIDE_BUY = 0
comptime SIDE_SELL = 1

comptime TYPE_LIMIT = 0
comptime TYPE_MARKET = 1
comptime TYPE_IOC = 2
comptime TYPE_FOK = 3

comptime STATE_NEW = 0
comptime STATE_ACKED = 1
comptime STATE_PARTIAL = 2
comptime STATE_FILLED = 3
comptime STATE_CANCELLED = 4
comptime STATE_REJECTED = 5
comptime STATE_UNKNOWN = -1

comptime PHASE_PREOPEN = 0
comptime PHASE_OPEN = 1
comptime PHASE_HALTED = 2
comptime PHASE_CLOSED = 3

comptime STP_NONE = 0
comptime STP_CANCEL_RESTING = 1
comptime STP_REJECT_INCOMING = 2

comptime NO_PRICE = -1


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Order: one instruction, with its lifecycle state carried on it.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

struct Order:
    var oid: Int
    var client_id: Int
    var side: Int
    var price: Int
    var qty: Int
    var remaining: Int
    var seq: Int
    var otype: Int
    var state: Int
    var ts_ns: Int

    def __init__(
        out self,
        oid: Int,
        client_id: Int,
        side: Int,
        price: Int,
        qty: Int,
        otype: Int,
        ts_ns: Int,
    ):
        self.oid = oid
        self.client_id = client_id
        self.side = side
        self.price = price
        self.qty = qty
        self.remaining = qty
        self.seq = 0
        self.otype = otype
        self.state = STATE_NEW
        self.ts_ns = ts_ns

    def is_buy(self) -> Bool:
        return self.side == SIDE_BUY

    def filled(self) -> Int:
        return self.qty - self.remaining

    def state_name(self) -> String:
        if self.state == STATE_NEW:
            return String("NEW")
        if self.state == STATE_ACKED:
            return String("ACKED")
        if self.state == STATE_PARTIAL:
            return String("PARTIAL")
        if self.state == STATE_FILLED:
            return String("FILLED")
        if self.state == STATE_CANCELLED:
            return String("CANCELLED")
        if self.state == STATE_REJECTED:
            return String("REJECTED")
        return String("UNKNOWN")

    def describe(self) -> String:
        var side_s = String("SELL")
        if self.side == SIDE_BUY:
            side_s = String("BUY ")
        return (
            String("oid=")
            + String(self.oid)
            + String(" ")
            + side_s
            + String(" px=")
            + String(self.price)
            + String(" qty=")
            + String(self.qty)
            + String(" rem=")
            + String(self.remaining)
            + String(" ")
            + self.state_name()
        )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# Trade: one fill on the tape. price is the resting order's price for
# continuous matching, and the clearing price for auction matching.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

struct Trade:
    var buy_oid: Int
    var sell_oid: Int
    var buy_client: Int
    var sell_client: Int
    var price: Int
    var qty: Int
    var seq: Int
    var auction: Bool

    def __init__(
        out self,
        buy_oid: Int,
        sell_oid: Int,
        buy_client: Int,
        sell_client: Int,
        price: Int,
        qty: Int,
        seq: Int,
        auction: Bool,
    ):
        self.buy_oid = buy_oid
        self.sell_oid = sell_oid
        self.buy_client = buy_client
        self.sell_client = sell_client
        self.price = price
        self.qty = qty
        self.seq = seq
        self.auction = auction

    def notional(self) -> Int:
        return self.price * self.qty

    def describe(self) -> String:
        var tag = String("C")
        if self.auction:
            tag = String("A")
        return (
            String("[")
            + tag
            + String("] seq=")
            + String(self.seq)
            + String(" buy_oid=")
            + String(self.buy_oid)
            + String(" sell_oid=")
            + String(self.sell_oid)
            + String(" px=")
            + String(self.price)
            + String(" qty=")
            + String(self.qty)
        )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# TradeReport: the drop-copy record of one fill. Immutable once
# published; report_seq numbers the reports in publication order.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

struct TradeReport:
    var report_seq: Int
    var trade_seq: Int
    var symbol: String
    var buy_oid: Int
    var sell_oid: Int
    var buy_client: Int
    var sell_client: Int
    var price: Int
    var qty: Int
    var auction: Bool

    def __init__(
        out self,
        report_seq: Int,
        trade_seq: Int,
        symbol: String,
        buy_oid: Int,
        sell_oid: Int,
        buy_client: Int,
        sell_client: Int,
        price: Int,
        qty: Int,
        auction: Bool,
    ):
        self.report_seq = report_seq
        self.trade_seq = trade_seq
        self.symbol = symbol
        self.buy_oid = buy_oid
        self.sell_oid = sell_oid
        self.buy_client = buy_client
        self.sell_client = sell_client
        self.price = price
        self.qty = qty
        self.auction = auction

    def describe(self) -> String:
        var tag = String("C")
        if self.auction:
            tag = String("A")
        return (
            String("report#")
            + String(self.report_seq)
            + String(" [")
            + tag
            + String("] trade_seq=")
            + String(self.trade_seq)
            + String(" ")
            + self.symbol
            + String(" buy_oid=")
            + String(self.buy_oid)
            + String(" sell_oid=")
            + String(self.sell_oid)
            + String(" px=")
            + String(self.price)
            + String(" qty=")
            + String(self.qty)
        )


# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------
# MatchingEngine.
# ---------------------------------------------------------------------------

struct MatchingEngine:
    var bids: List[Order]
    var asks: List[Order]
    var auction_bids: List[Order]
    var auction_asks: List[Order]
    var done: List[Order]
    var trades: List[Trade]
    var rejected_oids: List[Int]
    var reject_reasons: List[String]
    var clients: List[Int]
    var pos_qty: List[Int]
    var pos_cash: List[Int]
    var next_seq: Int
    var total_volume: Int
    var total_notional: Int
    var last_trade_price: Int
    var n_submitted: Int
    var n_rejected: Int
    var n_cancelled: Int
    var phase: Int
    var stp_mode: Int
    var reference_price: Int
    var last_stp_reject: Bool
    var symbol: String
    var reports: List[TradeReport]
    var next_report_seq: Int

    def __init__(out self):
        self.bids = List[Order]()
        self.asks = List[Order]()
        self.auction_bids = List[Order]()
        self.auction_asks = List[Order]()
        self.done = List[Order]()
        self.trades = List[Trade]()
        self.rejected_oids = List[Int]()
        self.reject_reasons = List[String]()
        self.clients = List[Int]()
        self.pos_qty = List[Int]()
        self.pos_cash = List[Int]()
        self.next_seq = 1
        self.total_volume = 0
        self.total_notional = 0
        self.last_trade_price = 0
        self.n_submitted = 0
        self.n_rejected = 0
        self.n_cancelled = 0
        self.phase = PHASE_PREOPEN
        self.stp_mode = STP_CANCEL_RESTING
        self.reference_price = 0
        self.last_stp_reject = False
        self.symbol = String("SIM")
        self.reports = List[TradeReport]()
        self.next_report_seq = 0

    def _take_seq(mut self) -> Int:
        var s = self.next_seq
        self.next_seq += 1
        return s

    def set_phase(mut self, phase: Int):
        self.phase = phase

    def set_stp_mode(mut self, mode: Int):
        self.stp_mode = mode

    def set_reference_price(mut self, price: Int):
        self.reference_price = price

    def set_symbol(mut self, sym: String):
        self.symbol = sym

    # -- position bookkeeping ----------------------------------------------
    # Find a client's slot, creating it at zero if this is the first trade.
    def _client_slot(mut self, client_id: Int) -> Int:
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.clients):
            if self.clients[i] == client_id:
                return i
            i += 1
        self.clients.append(client_id)
        self.pos_qty.append(0)
        self.pos_cash.append(0)
        return len(self.clients) - 1

    def _apply_trade(mut self, buy_client: Int, sell_client: Int, price: Int, qty: Int):
        var b = self._client_slot(buy_client)
        self.pos_qty[b] += qty
        self.pos_cash[b] -= price * qty
        var s = self._client_slot(sell_client)
        self.pos_qty[s] -= qty
        self.pos_cash[s] += price * qty

    def position_qty(self, client_id: Int) -> Int:
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.clients):
            if self.clients[i] == client_id:
                return self.pos_qty[i]
            i += 1
        return 0

    def position_cash(self, client_id: Int) -> Int:
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.clients):
            if self.clients[i] == client_id:
                return self.pos_cash[i]
            i += 1
        return 0

    # -- tape ---------------------------------------------------------------
    def _record_trade(
        mut self,
        buy_oid: Int,
        sell_oid: Int,
        buy_client: Int,
        sell_client: Int,
        price: Int,
        qty: Int,
        auction: Bool,
    ):
        var tseq = self._take_seq()
        var t = Trade(
            buy_oid, sell_oid, buy_client, sell_client, price, qty, tseq, auction
        )
        self.trades.append(t^)
        var r = TradeReport(
            self.next_report_seq,
            tseq,
            self.symbol,
            buy_oid,
            sell_oid,
            buy_client,
            sell_client,
            price,
            qty,
            auction,
        )
        self.next_report_seq += 1
        self.reports.append(r^)
        self.total_volume += qty
        self.total_notional += price * qty
        self.last_trade_price = price
        self._apply_trade(buy_client, sell_client, price, qty)

    # -- sorted insertion ----------------------------------------------------
    def _insert_bid(mut self, var order: Order):
        var pos = len(self.bids)
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.bids):
            if self.bids[i].price < order.price:
                pos = i
                break
            i += 1
        self.bids.insert(pos, order^)

    def _insert_ask(mut self, var order: Order):
        var pos = len(self.asks)
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.asks):
            if self.asks[i].price > order.price:
                pos = i
                break
            i += 1
        self.asks.insert(pos, order^)

    def _insert_auction_bid(mut self, var order: Order):
        var pos = len(self.auction_bids)
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.auction_bids):
            if self.auction_bids[i].price < order.price:
                pos = i
                break
            i += 1
        self.auction_bids.insert(pos, order^)

    def _insert_auction_ask(mut self, var order: Order):
        var pos = len(self.auction_asks)
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.auction_asks):
            if self.auction_asks[i].price > order.price:
                pos = i
                break
            i += 1
        self.auction_asks.insert(pos, order^)

    # -- rejection -----------------------------------------------------------
    def _reject(mut self, oid: Int, reason: String):
        self.rejected_oids.append(oid)
        self.reject_reasons.append(reason)
        self.n_rejected += 1

    def reject_reason(self, oid: Int) -> String:
        var i = 0
        while i < len(self.rejected_oids):
            if self.rejected_oids[i] == oid:
                return self.reject_reasons[i]
            i += 1
        return String("")

    # -- continuous matching -------------------------------------------------
    def _match_buy(mut self, mut order: Order, limit_price: Int) -> Int:
        var n_trades = 0
        while (
            order.remaining > 0
            and len(self.asks) > 0
            and self.asks[0].price <= limit_price
        ):
            # Self-trade check against the current best ask.
            if self.asks[0].client_id == order.client_id and self.stp_mode != STP_NONE:
                if self.stp_mode == STP_REJECT_INCOMING:
                    self.last_stp_reject = True
                    return n_trades
                var resting = self.asks.pop(0)
